```diff
-## 5. Matrix 1: Segment-by-Persona Commercial Angles
-
-This matrix defines the commercial discussion angle for each buying persona within the two approved ICP segments. Each angle is a discovery hypothesis informed by the business model and the persona's likely responsibilities. It is not an assertion that the business has a confirmed operational or financial problem.
-
-| ICP Segment          | Champion                                                                                                                                                                            | Economic Buyer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Influencer                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
-| -------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
-| `RETAIL_SUPERMARKET` | **Finance Manager / Financial Controller:** Explore how the finance function manages collections, POS settlements, reconciliation and financial visibility across existing outlets. | **CFO / Finance Director / MD / Owner:** Explore whether current banking and payment arrangements provide the financial control, settlement visibility and scalability required to support the supermarket's operating model and growth.      | **Head of Retail Operations / Operations Manager:** Explore how payment acceptance, collections and transaction-related processes are coordinated across outlets, and how operational teams work with central finance.                |
-| `FMCG_DISTRIBUTOR`   | **Finance Manager / Treasury Manager:** Explore how the finance function manages retailer collections, reconciliation, cash visibility and coordination of supplier payments.       | **CFO / Finance Director / MD / Owner:** Explore whether current banking and payment arrangements support collection efficiency, financial control, cash-flow visibility and supplier-payment coordination across the distribution operation. | **Head of Distribution Operations / Commercial Operations Manager:** Explore how collection and payment workflows connect sales teams, depots, distribution operations and central finance across the business's operating footprint. |
-
-### Matrix Interpretation Rules
+- **Physical Expansion:** Investigate how new locations affect payment acceptance, collections, cash handling and coordination with central finance.
+- **Fleet or Infrastructure Investment:** Explore the operational and financial workflows associated with the investment.
+- **Payment Infrastructure Changes:** Identify the operational teams responsible for implementation and day-to-day use.
+- **Finance and Treasury Recruitment:** Investigate how finance responsibilities interact with operational teams. Do not assume the operations leader is involved in recruitment decisions.
+- **New Supplier or Commercial Agreements:** Explore whether the agreements change distribution, collection, payment or operational coordination requirements.
+
+**Commercial discussion angle:** Explore how financial workflows connect central finance with outlets, warehouses, stations, distribution teams, fleets and other operating units.
+
+## 5. Segment-Specific Buying Committee
+
+The following matrix adapts the three buying roles to the seven approved ICP segments. These are research priorities, not assumptions that every organisation has the same structure.
+
+| ICP segment | Champion | Economic Buyer | Influencer |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| `FMCG_DISTRIBUTOR` | Finance Manager / Treasury Manager / Financial Controller | Owner / MD for mid-sized businesses; CFO / Finance Director for larger businesses | Head of Distribution Operations / Commercial Operations Manager |
+| `PETROL_STATION_NETWORK` | Finance Manager / Financial Controller / Treasury Manager | Owner / MD, particularly for small and mid-sized networks | Operations Director / Head of Retail Operations / Station Operations Manager |
+| `RETAIL_SUPERMARKET` | Finance Manager / Financial Controller | Owner / MD for smaller chains; CFO / Finance Director for larger groups | Head of Retail Operations / Regional Operations Manager |
+| `QSR_GROUP` | Finance Manager / Group Accountant / Financial Controller | Owner / MD / CEO / CFO | COO / Head of Restaurant Operations / Regional Operations Manager |
+| `PHARMACY_CHAIN` | Finance Manager / Financial Controller / Group Accountant | Owner / MD / CEO / CFO | Head of Pharmacy Operations / Retail Operations Manager |
+| `LOGISTICS_HAULAGE` | Finance Manager / Treasury Manager / Financial Controller | Owner / MD / CFO | Head of Fleet Operations / Transport Operations Manager |
+| `HEALTHCARE_DIAGNOSTICS` | Finance Manager / Financial Controller / Corporate Finance Manager | Owner / MD / CEO / CFO | COO / Centre Operations Manager / Head of Administration |
+
+### Segment-Specific Commercial Angles
+
+**A. FMCG Distributors and Wholesalers**
+
+- **Champion:** Explore how finance manages retailer collections, trade receivables, reconciliation, cash visibility and supplier payments.
+- **Economic Buyer:** Explore whether banking and payment arrangements support collection efficiency, liquidity management, financial control and supplier-payment coordination.
+- **Influencer:** Explore how collection and payment workflows connect sales teams, depots, distribution operations and central finance.
+
+Relevant signals to investigate include finance and treasury recruitment, warehouse or depot commissioning, geographic expansion and new supplier or distribution agreements.
+
+**B. Petrol Station Networks**
+
+- **Champion:** Explore how finance manages daily station collections, cash and POS reconciliation, settlement reporting and financial visibility across stations.
+- **Economic Buyer:** Explore whether banking and payment arrangements support daily liquidity, transaction volumes, financial control and station-network expansion.
+- **Influencer:** Explore how station operations coordinate cash handling, POS transactions, transfers, daily reporting and central finance.
+
+Relevant signals to investigate include new station commissioning, network expansion, CNG or EV infrastructure investment and diversified retail offerings.
+
+CNG or EV investment is an infrastructure and expansion indicator. It does not independently establish payment friction, settlement delays or demand for credit.
+
+**C. Retail Chains and Supermarkets**
+
+- **Champion:** Explore how finance manages collections, POS settlements, reconciliation and financial visibility across outlets.
+- **Economic Buyer:** Explore whether banking and payment arrangements provide the financial control, settlement visibility and scalability required to support the supermarket's operating model and growth.
+- **Influencer:** Explore how payment acceptance, collections and transaction-related processes are coordinated across outlets, and how operational teams work with central finance.
+
+Relevant signals to investigate include new branch openings, geographic expansion and recruitment for finance or financial-control roles.
+
+**D. Quick-Service Restaurant Groups**
+
+- **Champion:** Explore how finance manages collections from different outlets, payment reconciliation, settlement reporting and financial visibility.
+- **Economic Buyer:** Explore whether banking and payment arrangements support outlet-level financial control, transaction visibility and the group's expansion objectives.
+- **Influencer:** Explore how restaurant teams manage cash, POS and transfer transactions, and how outlet-level reporting reaches central finance.
+
+Relevant signals to investigate include multi-outlet expansion, finance recruitment and changes to centralised payment or POS management systems.
+
+**E. Pharmacy Chains**
+
+- **Champion:** Explore how finance manages collections, outlet reconciliation, supplier payments and financial reporting across pharmacy locations.
+- **Economic Buyer:** Explore whether banking and payment arrangements support financial control, operating scale and the group's commercial objectives.
+- **Influencer:** Explore how pharmacy operations coordinate outlet transactions, stock-related financial processes and reporting with central finance.
+
+Relevant signals to investigate include acquisitions, new pharmacy locations and recruitment for finance or accounting roles.
+
+Pharmacy-specific treasury maturity and customer or outlet churn remain research gaps. Do not present them as established buying triggers.
+
+**F. Logistics and Haulage**
+
+- **Champion:** Explore how finance manages customer collections, fleet-related expenditure, driver payments, supplier obligations and cash-flow visibility.
+- **Economic Buyer:** Explore whether banking arrangements support financial control, operational expenditure, collection efficiency and the financial requirements of fleet expansion.
+- **Influencer:** Explore how fleet and transport operations coordinate expenses, driver payments, customer collections and reporting with finance.
+
+Relevant signals to investigate include fleet expansion, new haulage contracts and expansion of distribution or transport capacity.
+
+Fleet investment does not independently establish demand for working-capital credit or a need to change banking providers.
+
+**G. Specialised Healthcare and Diagnostic Networks**
+
+- **Champion:** Explore how finance manages patient collections, corporate payments, reconciliation, billing-related financial reporting and cash visibility across centres.
+- **Economic Buyer:** Explore whether banking and payment arrangements support financial control, collection efficiency and the financial requirements of centre expansion.
+- **Influencer:** Explore how centre operations coordinate patient payments, billing workflows, transaction reporting and financial administration.
+
+Relevant signals to investigate include new diagnostic centres, geographic expansion, finance and treasury recruitment and investment in billing or payment infrastructure.
+
+Public-sector diagnostic infrastructure projects must not automatically be treated as private commercial prospects. Verify the ownership and operating entity before assigning the account to this segment.
+
+## 6. Signal-to-Persona Assignment Rules
+
```

```diff
-*   **Strongest Evidence:** FMCG distribution trade receivables trends (Q1 2026 data) and the impact of T+1 settlement on the downstream oil and gas sector [S029, S035].
-*   **Research Gaps:** Specific "churn" data or pain points for pharmacy chains beyond anecdotal evidence [S040].
-*   **Confidence Levels:**
-    *   **High:** FMCG/Supermarket fit for reconciliation.
-    *   **Medium:** Petrol station urgency for instant settlement (based on Moniepoint case study).
-    *   **Low:** Specific internal treasury maturity levels for private healthcare providers.
```

```diff
 # icp/signals.md
 
 ## 1. Research Scope and Methodology
+
 This report provides a systematic identification of buying signals for Moniepoint based on the approved ICP. It evaluates operational and financial events within seven designated segments to align with Moniepoint's core value propositions: instant settlement, working capital credit, and multi-location financial management. Methodology includes analyzing 49 sources for triggers of financial complexity and operational expansion.
 
 ## 2. Approved ICP Segments and Priority Classifications
-*   **PRIMARY:** FMCG Distributors, Petrol Station Networks, Retail Chains (Supermarkets).
-*   **SECONDARY:** Quick-Service Restaurant (QSR) Groups, Pharmacy Chains.
-*   **EMERGING:** Logistics/Haulage, Specialized Healthcare/Diagnostic Networks.

+
```

### Text Change

```diff
--- previous
+++ current
@@ -50,8 +50,35 @@
 
 ### 5. Anti-ICP and Exclusion Logic
 
-- **Definitively Unsuitable:** Sole-proprietor businesses with no evidence of formal accounting, digital payment infrastructure, or plans for multi-location scaling.
-- **Excluded Scope:** Highly informal "kiosk" businesses with zero electronic payment footprint, as they lack the transactional volume or reconciliation complexity to benefit from Moniepoint's core treasury/management tools [S018].
+## 5. Anti-ICP, Not-a-Fit Criteria and Exclusion Logic
+
+A business must satisfy the relevant segment's mandatory qualification conditions before entering the qualified-account pipeline.
+
+Industry membership alone does not establish ICP fit.
+
+| Not-a-fit category | Exclusion reason | Qualification decision |
+|---|---|---|
+| Single-location microbusinesses without evidence of scaling | Insufficient evidence of multi-location financial complexity or significant transaction coordination requirements. | Exclude unless other documented evidence establishes meaningful transaction volume and operational complexity. |
+| Informal sole proprietors without demonstrable financial infrastructure | No verified evidence of formal financial processes, digital payment infrastructure or scaling requirements. | Exclude unless additional evidence establishes a commercially relevant operating model. |
+| Petrol stations operating only one location | Does not meet the approved three-station minimum for the petrol station network segment. | Exclude from `PETROL_STATION_NETWORK`. |
+| FMCG traders without significant warehousing or distribution operations | Trading activity alone does not establish the depot, warehousing, receivables or distribution complexity required by the ICP. | Exclude unless mandatory distribution and operational conditions are verified. |
+| Retail businesses with no verifiable branch network | A retail business is not automatically a retail chain. | Exclude from `RETAIL_SUPERMARKET` unless the approved multi-location requirements are met. |
+| Businesses with announced but unverified expansion | Intended expansion does not establish an operationally active or committed change in business scale. | Retain for research; do not qualify on the announcement alone. |
+| Dormant, inactive or permanently closed businesses | No credible evidence of ongoing commercial operations. | Exclude. |
+| Companies without a verifiable Nigerian operating entity | The project targets qualified Nigerian businesses and requires a verifiable operating entity. | Exclude unless the Nigerian operating entity can be established. |
+| Public-sector infrastructure projects without a qualifying private operating entity | Public infrastructure investment does not automatically create a private-sector commercial prospect. | Exclude from private-sector targeting unless a qualifying commercial operator is independently verified. |
+| Businesses whose operations fall outside the approved ICP segments | Industry adjacency alone does not establish an approved segment fit. | Exclude from the current campaign or retain separately for future ICP research. |
+
+### Exclusion safeguards
+
+- Do not disqualify a business simply because a relevant finance contact has not yet been identified.
+- Do not treat the absence of public payment-friction evidence as proof that the business has no payment problem.
+- Do not qualify a business merely because it has recruited a finance professional.
+- Do not treat an announced expansion as a completed expansion.
+- Do not confuse a business's industry with its actual operating model.
+- Do not infer Moniepoint product suitability or credit eligibility from ICP qualification alone.
+
+**Decision principle:** An account must satisfy the approved ICP's segment-specific requirements. Buying signals and personas support prioritisation and research; neither can override an exclusion or substitute for mandatory qualification.
 
 ---
 
```

### Text Change

```diff
@@ -45,7 +45,238 @@
 - **Moderate:** CNG infrastructure investment (Indicates capital intensive growth) [S033].
 - **Unvalidated Hypothesis:** Pharmacy churn rates or specific internal treasury maturity levels [S040].
 
-
+## 8. Four-Test Buying Signal Evaluation Framework
+
+Every buying signal must be evaluated against four commercial and operational tests before it is selected for automated detection or campaign prioritisation.
+
+### 8.1 Evaluation Criteria
+
+| Test | Assessment question | STRONG | MODERATE | WEAK |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| Clear buying reason | Does it explain why now? | Directly indicates a material change that could create a relevant financial or operational requirement. | Indicates a possible change or requirement, but the commercial implication needs investigation. | Has little demonstrable connection to a current commercial requirement. |
+| Enough volume | Does it fire often enough to fill a pipeline? | Likely to occur frequently across the approved market. | Occurs across a meaningful subset of accounts but may constrain pipeline volume. | Rare, highly concentrated or unlikely to produce sufficient qualified accounts. |
+| Data you can get | Can a tool detect it reliably? | Observable through accessible, repeatable public data or structured sources. | Detectable, but requires multiple sources, manual verification or inconsistent data. | Difficult to observe consistently or dependent on inaccessible information. |
+| A natural story | Does it provide an honest first line? | Creates a credible, specific and relevant reason to investigate the company's financial or operational requirements. | Provides a reasonable opening, but requires additional context before commercial relevance is established. | Produces a generic, speculative or misleading opening. |
+
+### 8.2 Signal Scorecard
+
+The following assessments apply to the Nigerian market and the approved Moniepoint ICP. They are strategic research assessments, not measured conversion rates or verified market-frequency statistics.
+
+| Buying signal | Clear buying reason | Enough volume | Data you can get | A natural story |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| Finance/Treasury recruitment | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG | STRONG |
+| Warehouse/depot commissioning | STRONG | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Geographic distribution expansion | STRONG | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| New supplier/distribution agreements | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| New petrol station commissioning | STRONG | MODERATE | STRONG | STRONG |
+| CNG/EV infrastructure investment | MODERATE | WEAK | MODERATE | MODERATE |
+| Non-fuel retail diversification | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Supermarket branch expansion | STRONG | MODERATE | STRONG | STRONG |
+| QSR multi-outlet expansion | STRONG | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Centralised POS/payment-system deployment | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Pharmacy acquisitions | STRONG | WEAK | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Pharmacy finance/accounting recruitment | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Fleet expansion | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG | STRONG |
+| New haulage contracts | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| New diagnostic-centre expansion | MODERATE | WEAK | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Billing/payment infrastructure investment | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Payment infrastructure transition | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+| Explicit settlement/reconciliation friction | STRONG | WEAK | WEAK | STRONG |
+| Ownership or management change | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE |
+| Finance/internal-audit recruitment in multi-location retail | MODERATE | MODERATE | MODERATE | STRONG |
+
+### 8.3 Interpretation of Individual Signals
+
+**Finance/Treasury Recruitment**
+
+A finance or treasury vacancy can reveal investment in financial oversight and an identifiable finance function.
+
+However, the vacancy does not establish that the company intends to change banking providers or that the new employee has purchasing authority.
+
+The signal becomes more useful when the advertised responsibilities relate to treasury, cash management, collections, reconciliation or multi-location financial control.
+
+**Warehouse/Depot Commissioning**
+
+A new warehouse or depot can increase the number of operating locations, financial workflows and coordination requirements.
+
+Verify that the facility belongs to the target company and is operational or demonstrably commissioned.
+
+**Geographic Distribution Expansion**
+
+Expansion into new regions can create additional collection, supplier-payment and financial coordination requirements.
+
+Verify actual operating expansion rather than relying on broad growth announcements.
+
+**Supplier/Distribution Agreements**
+
+New commercial agreements can change purchasing obligations, distribution responsibilities and trade-payment arrangements.
+
+An agreement does not independently establish payment friction or increased credit demand.
+
+**Petrol Station Commissioning**
+
+New stations increase the potential number of payment-acceptance and reconciliation locations.
+
+The signal becomes commercially stronger when the network has multiple operating stations and evidence of relevant transaction complexity.
+
+**CNG/EV Investment**
+
+Infrastructure investment can indicate business expansion and capital deployment.
+
+It does not establish a payment problem, immediate liquidity shortage or eligibility for working-capital facilities.
+
+**Non-Fuel Retail Diversification**
+
+Convenience stores, restaurants and other station-based offerings can increase transaction types and operating complexity.
+
+Investigate whether these activities are operated by the same commercial entity.
+
+**Supermarket Branch Expansion**
+
+New branches create a reason to investigate centralised financial reporting, collections and settlement coordination.
+
+Verify the actual outlet opening and whether it belongs to the same operating company.
+
+**QSR Multi-Outlet Expansion**
+
+Additional restaurant locations can increase the complexity of outlet collections and consolidated financial reporting.
+
+A single new outlet does not automatically establish significant financial complexity.
+
+**Centralised POS/Payment-System Deployment**
+
+A documented systems deployment creates an opportunity to investigate payment acceptance, settlement reporting and integration with finance.
+
+Distinguish the software vendor's announcement from verified adoption by the target business.
+
+**Pharmacy Acquisitions**
+
+Acquisitions can introduce additional operating locations, supplier relationships and financial integration requirements.
+
+Verify the acquiring entity and whether the acquired outlets remain under its operational control.
+
+**Fleet Expansion**
+
+Additional vehicles can increase operating expenditure, driver-payment coordination and fleet-related financial administration.
+
+Fleet investment alone does not prove demand for credit.
+
+**Haulage Contracts**
+
+New contracts can change collection schedules, operating expenditure and payment obligations.
+
+Verify the contract, the contracting entity and whether the company is the service provider or customer.
+
+**Diagnostic-Centre Expansion**
+
+New centres can create additional patient-collection, corporate-payment and billing-related reporting requirements.
+
+Verify the private operating entity and distinguish commercial operators from public-sector projects.
+
+**Billing/Payment Infrastructure Investment**
+
+A documented investment in billing or payment infrastructure provides a reason to investigate how the organisation coordinates collections and financial reporting.
+
+It does not automatically indicate dissatisfaction with existing banking arrangements.
+
+**Payment Infrastructure Transition**
+
+A verified change in payment acceptance or processing arrangements can indicate active financial infrastructure evaluation.
+
+Establish what changed, when it changed and which entity made the decision.
+
+**Explicit Settlement/Reconciliation Friction**
+
+Public statements about settlement delays, reconciliation failures or payment difficulties can provide a particularly relevant commercial opening.
+
+However, these statements are less frequently available and may not identify the affected company's current circumstances.
+
+Do not transfer an industry-wide problem to an individual account without account-level evidence.
+
+**Ownership/Management Change**
+
+A change in ownership or leadership can create an opportunity to investigate financial governance and banking relationships.
+
+Do not assume that new management intends to replace existing financial service providers.
+
+### 8.4 Signal Selection Decision
+
+Two signals receive priority for initial campaign implementation.
+
+| Selection | Signal | Decision | Reason |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| PRIMARY | `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT` | Select for initial automated detection | Strong detectability and a credible conversation around financial oversight, despite moderate scores on buying urgency and pipeline volume. |
+| SECONDARY | `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION` | Select for secondary automated detection and account enrichment | Strong buying reason and credible commercial relevance, with moderate volume and detection reliability. |
+
+The selection is based on the combined usefulness of the four tests, not on an assumption that every detected account is ready to purchase.
+
+### 8.5 Initial Automation Requirements
+
+**FINANCE_RECRUITMENT**
+
+Recommended detection terms:
+
+- Finance Manager
+- Financial Controller
+- Treasury Manager
+- Head of Finance
+- Group Accountant
+- Finance Operations Manager
+- Treasury Lead
+
+Verification requirements:
+
+1. Confirm that the vacancy belongs to the target company.
+2. Verify that the position is current.
+3. Extract the responsibilities and seniority.
+4. Determine whether the role appears newly created or replacement-based.
+5. Establish whether the responsibilities relate to the ICP's financial workflows.
+6. Record the source and detection date.
+
+**MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION**
+
+Recommended detection events:
+
+- New branch openings.
+- New petrol station commissioning.
+- New warehouses or depots.
+- Geographic distribution expansion.
+- New diagnostic or pharmacy locations.
+- Multi-outlet restaurant expansion.
+
+Verification requirements:
+
+1. Confirm the new location or expansion event.
+2. Establish the operating company's identity.
+3. Verify that the location is attributable to the target account.
+4. Distinguish actual openings from proposed expansion.
+5. Record the previous and current known location counts where available.
+6. Record the source and detection date.
+
+### 8.6 Signal Interpretation Safeguards
+
+- A signal is not ICP qualification.
+- A signal is not proof of purchase intent.
+- A signal is not proof of a payment or settlement problem.
+- A signal is not proof that the company wants to change banking providers.
+- A signal is not proof of Moniepoint product or credit eligibility.
+- Multiple signals increase research priority only when independently verified.
+- Signal strength must remain separate from persona confidence and account qualification.
+- Missing public evidence must not be represented as evidence that a problem does not exist.
+
+### 8.7 Relationship to Personas and Workflow
+
+`icp.md` establishes whether the company qualifies.
+
+`signals.md` establishes what observable events justify further investigation and which signals merit automation.
+
+`personas.md` establishes which buying roles and commercial angles should be researched.
+
+`workflow.md` governs detection, verification, account routing, evidence capture, human review and outreach approval.
+
+The resulting sequence is:
+
+`ICP QUALIFICATION → SIGNAL DETECTION → SIGNAL SCORING → ACCOUNT ROUTING → PERSONA IDENTIFICATION → HUMAN REVIEW → OUTREACH APPROVAL`
 
 ## 8. High-Value Signal Combinations
 
```

```diff
 ## 5. Anti-ICP, Not-a-Fit Criteria and Exclusion Logic
 
 A business must satisfy the relevant segment's mandatory qualification conditions before entering the qualified-account pipeline.
 
 Industry membership alone does not establish ICP fit.
 
-| Not-a-fit category | Exclusion reason | Qualification decision |
-|---|---|---|
-| Single-location microbusinesses without evidence of scaling | Insufficient evidence of multi-location financial complexity or significant transaction coordination requirements. | Exclude unless other documented evidence establishes meaningful transaction volume and operational complexity. |
-| Informal sole proprietors without demonstrable financial infrastructure | No verified evidence of formal financial processes, digital payment infrastructure or scaling requirements. | Exclude unless additional evidence establishes a commercially relevant operating model. |
-| Petrol stations operating only one location | Does not meet the approved three-station minimum for the petrol station network segment. | Exclude from `PETROL_STATION_NETWORK`. |
-| FMCG traders without significant warehousing or distribution operations | Trading activity alone does not establish the depot, warehousing, receivables or distribution complexity required by the ICP. | Exclude unless mandatory distribution and operational conditions are verified. |
-| Retail businesses with no verifiable branch network | A retail business is not automatically a retail chain. | Exclude from `RETAIL_SUPERMARKET` unless the approved multi-location requirements are met. |
-| Businesses with announced but unverified expansion | Intended expansion does not establish an operationally active or committed change in business scale. | Retain for research; do not qualify on the announcement alone. |
-| Dormant, inactive or permanently closed businesses | No credible evidence of ongoing commercial operations. | Exclude. |
-| Companies without a verifiable Nigerian operating entity | The project targets qualified Nigerian businesses and requires a verifiable operating entity. | Exclude unless the Nigerian operating entity can be established. |
-| Public-sector infrastructure projects without a qualifying private operating entity | Public infrastructure investment does not automatically create a private-sector commercial prospect. | Exclude from private-sector targeting unless a qualifying commercial operator is independently verified. |
-| Businesses whose operations fall outside the approved ICP segments | Industry adjacency alone does not establish an approved segment fit. | Exclude from the current campaign or retain separately for future ICP research. |
+| Not-a-fit category                                                                  | Exclusion reason                                                                                                              | Qualification decision                                                                                         |

+| Single-location microbusinesses without evidence of scaling                         | Insufficient evidence of multi-location financial complexity or significant transaction coordination requirements.            | Exclude unless other documented evidence establishes meaningful transaction volume and operational complexity. |
```

```diff
+# personas.md
+
+## 1. Purpose and Operating Principles
+
+This document defines the buying committee to investigate within businesses that have passed the ICP qualification process.
+
+It translates the approved ICP segments and detected buying signals into three commercial research roles:
+
+1. **CHAMPION:** The person most likely to understand the financial requirement, evaluate potential solutions and advocate for internal consideration.
+2. **ECONOMIC_BUYER:** The person with verified authority to approve the banking relationship, commercial expenditure or final purchasing decision.
+3. **INFLUENCER:** The person whose operational, technical or functional responsibilities shape requirements, evaluation, implementation or adoption.
+
+These are functional buying roles, not fixed job titles.
+
+One individual may occupy multiple roles, particularly in smaller businesses. A senior title does not automatically establish budget authority, and a finance title does not automatically establish purchasing influence.
+
+### Operating sequence
+
+`ICP QUALIFICATION → SIGNAL DETECTION → ACCOUNT ROUTING → PERSONA IDENTIFICATION → HUMAN REVIEW → OUTREACH APPROVAL`
+
+Persona identification must follow account qualification and signal research.
+
+An account can qualify even when no relevant contact has been identified.
+
+A detected signal provides a reason to investigate an account. It does not automatically establish that a particular person is a buyer or that the business intends to purchase a financial product.
 
 ## 2. Champion
 
-**Primary persona:** Finance Manager / Treasury Manager / Financial Controller
+**Primary persona:** Finance Manager / Treasury Manager / Financial Controller.
 
 **Alternative titles:**
 
@@ -30,114 +34,110 @@
 - Finance Operations Manager
 - Treasury Lead
 - Financial Operations Lead
+- Group Accountant
 - Senior Accountant, where relevant responsibilities are verified
-- Group Accountant
 - Finance Director, where the individual also performs the champion function
 
-**Organisational position:** Finance-function professional responsible for financial processes, reporting, cash management, collections, payments or reconciliation.
-
-**Relevant responsibilities:**
-
-- Overseeing collections, payments and financial transactions.
-- Monitoring cash flow and financial positions.

+**Organisational function:** Financial management, treasury, collections, payments, reconciliation and financial control.
+
+### Responsibilities to investigate
+
+- Managing collections and outgoing payments.
+- Monitoring cash flow and liquidity.
+- Coordinating settlement and reconciliation processes.
 - Managing banking relationships and financial service processes.
-- Supporting financial controls and operational efficiency.
+- Maintaining financial controls and reporting.
+- Coordinating financial administration across branches, stations, warehouses or operating centres.
 - Providing financial information to senior management.
-- Coordinating financial processes across branches, depots or operating locations.
-
-**Potential priorities to investigate:**
+
+### Potential priorities
 
 - Visibility into incoming and outgoing funds.
-- Reconciliation of collections and settlements.

-- Consistent financial reporting across operating locations.
-- Reliable banking services for business operations.
-- Efficient financial administration and reporting.

-**Discovery hypotheses, not assumed problems:**
-
-- Whether financial visibility is fragmented across locations.
-- Whether collections require manual reconciliation.
-- How supplier payments and other financial obligations are coordinated.
-- Whether settlement timing affects cash availability.
-- Whether financial processes scale effectively with the operating footprint.

-

-
-- **Finance and Treasury Recruitment:** A relevant vacancy indicates recruitment for a finance function. Investigate the advertised responsibilities, reporting structure and whether the role is newly created or replacing an existing employee. Do not automatically classify the vacancy holder as the champion.
-- **Multi-Location Expansion:** Explore whether additional branches, stations, depots or centres affect collections, settlements, reconciliation and financial visibility.
-- **Infrastructure or Fleet Investment:** Investigate whether the investment creates additional payment, cash-flow or financial coordination requirements.
-- **Supplier Agreements and Distribution Changes:** Explore whether new commercial arrangements affect collections, supplier payments, trade receivables or working-capital coordination.
-- **Payment Infrastructure Changes:** Investigate whether the business is changing its payment acceptance, settlement or reconciliation processes.
-- **Explicit Payment or Reconciliation Friction:** Where a company publicly identifies settlement delays, failed payments or reconciliation difficulties, investigate the finance function's involvement and the scale of the issue.
-
-**Commercial discussion angle:** Explore how the finance function manages collections, settlements, reconciliation, supplier payments and financial visibility across the company's operating footprint.
+- Reliable collection and settlement processes.

+- Coordination of supplier payments and other obligations.
+- Financial reporting and operational control.

+
+### Discovery hypotheses
+

+
+- Financial information is fragmented across operating locations.

+- Settlement timing affects cash availability.
+- Supplier payments depend on predictable collection cycles.
+- Existing banking arrangements support the company's transaction volumes.
+- Financial administration becomes more complex as the business expands.
+
+These are questions to investigate, not assumed customer problems.
+
+### Commercial angle
+
+Explore how the finance function manages collections, settlements, reconciliation, supplier payments and financial visibility across the company's operating footprint.
+
+**Why this persona matters:** The champion can explain the existing financial workflow, identify relevant requirements and help establish whether a banking or payments solution merits internal consideration.

+### Commercial angle
+
+Explore whether the company's banking and payments arrangements support its liquidity management, financial control, operating scale and commercial growth objectives.
+

+
```

```diff
@@ -1,813 +1,909 @@
-# Workflow Specification: Moniepoint Business Banking and Payments
-
-## Finance and Treasury Recruitment + Multi-Branch Expansion
-
-## 1. Workflow Overview
-
-- **Workflow:** ICP qualification and signal-based account prioritisation
-- **Market:** Nigerian retail supermarkets and FMCG distributors
-- **Primary signal:** `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT`
-- **Secondary signal:** `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION`
-- **Platform:** Clay, public-source research and downstream CRM preparation
-- **Purpose:** Identify businesses that meet the approved ICP and demonstrate observable developments that create a credible reason to investigate their banking, collections, settlements and financial operations.
-- **Automation boundary:** Discover, verify, qualify, research, classify, prioritise and prepare accounts for human review. Do not manufacture purchase intent, assert unverified operational problems or initiate outreach without approval.
-- **Build status:** Specification. The existing Python research foundation supports public-source research and documentation but is not yet an operational Clay workflow.
-
-The workflow translates the approved ICP and buying-signal research into an account-level operating process. It defines qualification rules, evidence requirements, research execution, routing, commercial interpretation, data structures and human approval.
-
-The ICP is the authority for account eligibility. The signal definitions determine which observable developments are investigated and how qualified accounts are prioritised.
+# Moniepoint Business Banking & Payments
+# GTM Signal Detection and Account Routing Workflow
+
+## 1. Workflow Status Line
+
+**STATUS:** Initial implementation specification | **MARKET:** Nigeria | **PREFERRED SIGNAL:** `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT` | **SECONDARY SIGNAL:** `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION` | **ICP GATE:** Approved ICP qualification before signal-based prioritisation | **OUTPUT:** Verified, enriched and commercially routed accounts ready for human review.
+
+| Attribute | Specification |
+|---|---|
+| Workflow name | Moniepoint Business Banking and Payments GTM Signal Workflow |
+| Workflow status | Initial implementation specification |
+| Preferred automated signal | `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT` |
+| Supporting signal | `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION` |
+| Primary ICP segments | FMCG Distributors, Petrol Station Networks, Retail Chains (Supermarkets) |
+| Secondary ICP segments | QSR Groups, Pharmacy Chains |
+| Emerging ICP segments | Logistics/Haulage, Specialized Healthcare/Diagnostic Networks |
+| Market | Nigeria |
+| Commercial themes | Instant settlement, working capital, multi-location financial management |
+| Execution platform | Clay |
+| ICP authority | `icp.md` |
+| Signal authority | `signals.md` |
+| Workflow authority | This document |
+
+**Operating rule:** Finance recruitment is the principal detection and routing signal. Multi-branch expansion provides a secondary signal that can strengthen account prioritisation or independently support a segment-specific commercial angle. Neither signal can override ICP qualification.
+
+---
 
 ## 2. Mother Intent
 
-Identify Nigerian businesses that:
-
-1. Operate within one of the two approved ICP segments.
-2. Pass all six mandatory ICP qualification conditions.
-3. Have a credible business model involving commercial collections, settlements or supplier-payment requirements.
-4. Demonstrate one or both selected observable developments:
-   - Finance and treasury recruitment.
-   - Multi-branch or geographic expansion.
-
-Use verified developments to prioritise qualified businesses for commercial investigation of Moniepoint Business Banking and Payments.
-
-A detected signal is a reason to investigate. It is not proof of payment friction, dissatisfaction with an existing financial provider or intent to purchase.
-
-Every account must distinguish:
-
-- Verified company facts.
-- ICP qualification decisions.
-- Source-backed signal evidence.
-- Commercial interpretation.
-- Unverified assumptions.
-- Purchasing intent, where independently established.
-
-## 3. Authoritative ICP and Signal Scope
-
-### 3.1 Approved ICP segments
-
-Only the following segments are eligible for this workflow.
-
-| Segment              | Inclusion definition                                                                                               |
-| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
-| `RETAIL_SUPERMARKET` | Businesses operating physical supermarket or grocery retail outlets.                                               |
-| `FMCG_DISTRIBUTOR`   | Businesses wholesaling or distributing fast-moving consumer goods to retailers, resellers or commercial customers. |
-
-Businesses outside these definitions are excluded from this workflow, even if they demonstrate a potentially relevant commercial development.
-
-This includes petrol stations, restaurants, pharmacies, healthcare providers, logistics companies, unrelated SMEs and online-only retailers.
-
-Research into other industries may inform broader market understanding. It does not independently authorise their inclusion in the operational ICP.
-
-An independent supermarket is not automatically excluded because it operates one location. It must be assessed against the same six mandatory ICP conditions as every other candidate.
-
-### 3.2 Active buying signals
-
-| Signal                   | Priority  | Active research definition                                                                                              | Recency            |
-| ------------------------ | --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------ |
-| `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT`    | Primary   | Attributable recruitment for relevant finance, treasury, accounting, financial control or financial operations roles.   | Previous 180 days  |
-| `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION` | Secondary | Specific opening, announced opening or geographic expansion of physical supermarket outlets or distribution operations. | Previous 12 months |
-
-These are the only two signals used in the workflow's account-level routing logic.
-
-Other indicators discussed in market research, including warehouse operations, logistics recruitment, general operational hiring, CNG investment, technology adoption and broad industry growth, are not independent routing signals.
-
-Such information may be retained as contextual research where relevant. It must not be used to manufacture a detected active signal or override the approved ICP.
-
-### 3.3 Separation of ICP fit and signal detection
-
-ICP qualification and signal detection are separate assessments.
-
-- A business can qualify without either signal being detected.
-- A business cannot qualify because it has a strong signal.
-- A detected signal cannot compensate for a failed ICP condition.
-- Signal absence does not automatically disqualify a qualified business.
-- Unknown evidence must remain unknown.
-
-## 4. Workflow Status and Progression
-
-### 4.1 Normal progression
-
-`DISCOVERED → IDENTITY_VERIFIED → ICP_SCREENED → ICP_QUALIFIED → SIGNAL_RESEARCH → SIGNAL_REVIEWED → ROUTED → HUMAN_REVIEW → APPROVED → CRM_READY`
-
-### 4.2 Status definitions
-
-| Status              | Entry condition and required action                                                                |
-| ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
-| `DISCOVERED`        | Candidate business identified. Create a provisional account record.                                |
-| `IDENTITY_VERIFIED` | Correct business identity and Nigerian operations supported by evidence. Check for duplicates.     |
-| `ICP_SCREENED`      | Evaluate all six mandatory ICP conditions independently.                                           |
-| `ICP_QUALIFIED`     | All six mandatory conditions pass. Initiate both signal searches.                                  |
-| `SIGNAL_RESEARCH`   | Finance recruitment and expansion searches are initiated and tracked independently.                |
-| `SIGNAL_REVIEWED`   | Both searches have terminal outcomes. Validate evidence and coverage before routing.               |
-| `ROUTED`            | Apply deterministic routing rules using the two signal outcomes.                                   |
-| `HUMAN_REVIEW`      | Reviewer assesses qualification, evidence, commercial angle and proposed next action.              |
-| `APPROVED`          | Human reviewer approves the account and proposed next action.                                      |
-| `CRM_READY`         | Required fields and human approval are present. Account is eligible for authorised CRM processing. |
-
-### 4.3 Exception statuses
-
-`ICP_DISQUALIFIED`, `FURTHER_RESEARCH`, `RESEARCH_BLOCKED`, `DUPLICATE`, `EVIDENCE_REJECTED`, `PROCESSING_ERROR`, `HUMAN_REJECTED`, `ON_HOLD`.
-
-### 4.4 Status controls
-
-- `workflow_status` represents lifecycle progression.
-- `icp_outcome` represents ICP qualification.
-- `signal_search_status` represents research execution.
-- `signal_outcome` represents the result of an individual signal search.
-- `routing_outcome` represents the next commercial action.
-- These fields must not substitute for one another.
-- An ICP-qualified account with no detected signals remains eligible for monitoring.
-- A failed or incomplete search must never be recorded as `NOT_DETECTED`.
-- Both signal searches must reach terminal outcomes before the account reaches `SIGNAL_REVIEWED`.
-- Terminal search execution does not automatically mean a signal was detected or absent.
-- AI-generated recommendations cannot approve an account.
-- `CRM_READY` means approved for CRM processing, not that synchronisation has occurred.
-
-## 5. ICP Qualification
-
-An account must pass all six mandatory ICP conditions before signal research begins.
-
-### 5.1 Mandatory qualification conditions
-
-Each condition must receive exactly one outcome: `PASS`, `FAIL` or `UNKNOWN`.
-
-| Condition                | PASS                                                                                                               | FAIL                                                                           | UNKNOWN                                                                         |
-| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
-| Nigerian presence        | Current Nigerian business operations supported by evidence.                                                        | No Nigerian operations established or relevant operations definitively ceased. | Nigerian presence cannot be established or reliably ruled out.                  |
-| Business identity        | Correct operating entity distinguishable through credible sources.                                                 | Candidate is the wrong entity or identity is disproved.                        | Available information is insufficient to establish the correct entity.          |
-| Segment fit              | Business activity matches `RETAIL_SUPERMARKET` or `FMCG_DISTRIBUTOR`.                                              | Business operates outside both approved segments.                              | Available evidence does not establish which approved segment applies.           |
-| Active operations        | Current commercial activity supported by evidence.                                                                 | Business is dormant or relevant operations have ceased.                        | Evidence is stale, contradictory or insufficient to establish current activity. |
-| Physical operating model | Physical retail outlets or genuine wholesale/distribution operations supported by evidence.                        | Exclusively online retail or no qualifying physical business operations.       | Physical operating model cannot be established.                                 |
-| Payments relevance       | Business model supports meaningful recurring commercial collections, settlements or supplier-payment requirements. | No meaningful relevance to the proposition is established.                     | Nature or significance of its payment activity cannot be established.           |
-
-### 5.2 Qualification decision rules
-
-- `QUALIFIED`: All six mandatory conditions are `PASS`.
-- `DISQUALIFIED`: At least one mandatory condition is definitively `FAIL`.
-- `FURTHER_RESEARCH`: No condition is `FAIL`, but at least one remains `UNKNOWN`.
-
-Missing information must never be treated as evidence of failure.
-
-A strong signal cannot compensate for failed ICP fit.
-
-### 5.3 Evidence requirements
-
-Every `PASS` and `FAIL` must have supporting evidence.
-
-Record:
-
-- Source name.
-- Direct source URL.
-- Specific fact being established.
-- Date accessed or verified.
-- Short explanation of why the evidence supports the decision.
-
-Prefer company-owned sources and credible independent publications.
-
-A search-result snippet alone is insufficient where the underlying source can be accessed.
-
-For active operations, use evidence of current trading or operating activity. An old company profile or undated directory entry does not independently establish that a business is still operating.
-
-For physical operations, record actual evidence of outlets, warehouses, depots or other relevant facilities. Do not infer a physical footprint from industry classification alone.
-
-For payments relevance, assess the business model and its transaction activities. The existence of commercial payments does not prove payment friction, poor banking service, reconciliation problems or purchase intent.
-
-### 5.4 Qualification boundaries
-
-- Five or more physical locations is a prioritisation heuristic, not a mandatory ICP condition.
-- A single-location supermarket can qualify if it passes all six conditions.
-- An FMCG distributor does not need to operate retail outlets.
-- A distributor's warehouse or depot can establish a physical operating model without being classified as a supermarket branch.
-- High transaction volume, payment complexity and consolidated payment-management requirements must be evidenced before being described as established account characteristics.
-- Finance recruitment is a signal, not a mandatory qualification condition.
-- No signal can override an ICP failure.
-
-### 5.5 Fit tier
-
-Assign a fit tier only after ICP qualification.
-
-| Fit tier         | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
-| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
-| `EXCELLENT_FIT`  | Strong evidence of substantial operating scale, such as five or more physical locations or a significant distribution footprint, alongside credible high-frequency transaction activity and relevant financial operations complexity. |
-| `REASONABLE_FIT` | Qualified business with a credible payment-management use case, including a single-location high-volume retailer or an FMCG distributor with meaningful B2B collection or supplier-payment activity.                                  |
-| `UNASSESSED`     | Evidence is insufficient to determine relative commercial attractiveness.                                                                                                                                                             |
-
-Fit tier is a prioritisation assessment. It must not change the mandatory ICP outcome.
-
-## 6. Signal Research and Validation
-
-Both active signals are researched independently for every ICP-qualified account.
-
-The objective is to identify observable events, retain source evidence and distinguish verified developments from search results that merely appear relevant.
-
-The wider research in `signals.md` informs signal selection and market understanding. It does not eliminate the need to verify each account-level event independently.
-
-### 6.1 Finance and Treasury Recruitment
-
-**Signal:** `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT`
-
-**Research objective:** Identify recent, attributable recruitment activity for finance, treasury or financial operations roles at the target business or a verified subsidiary.
-
-Eligible roles include:
-
-- Treasury Manager.
-- Finance Manager.
-- Financial Controller.
-- Finance Operations Manager.
-- Finance Analyst.
-- Relevant accounting, treasury and financial control roles.
-
-A vacancy qualifies only when the following can be established:
-
-1. Employer attribution matches the target account or a verified subsidiary.
-2. Role responsibilities are substantively relevant to finance, treasury, accounting, financial control or financial operations.
-3. Publication date falls within the previous 180 days.
-4. Vacancy status is established sufficiently to assess whether the recruitment is active.
-5. A direct source URL and supporting evidence are retained.
-
-A job title alone is insufficient. Generic job-category pages and vacancies without attributable employers do not qualify.
-
-**Vacancy status**
-
-`OPEN`, `CLOSED`, `EXPIRED`, `UNKNOWN`.
-
-An unknown vacancy status is not proof of active recruitment.
-
-**Candidate source categories**
-
-- Company careers and recruitment pages.
-- Verified company recruitment announcements.
-- Credible job boards and recruitment platforms.
-- Credible business and professional publications reporting specific vacancies.
-
-**Required evidence fields**
-
+Identify Nigerian businesses within Moniepoint's approved ICP that are investing in financial oversight or expanding their operations, then use those observable developments to identify credible opportunities for commercial conversations about business banking and payments.
+
+The preferred signal, `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT`, is intended to identify businesses recruiting for finance, treasury, financial control or related responsibilities. These vacancies can indicate investment in financial oversight and may provide a relevant reason to investigate how the business manages:
+
+- Collections and cash management.
+- Cash, POS and transfer reconciliation.
+- Treasury and supplier-payment operations.
+- Financial control across operating locations.
+- Working-capital coordination.
+- Consolidated financial reporting.
+
+`MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION` provides supporting evidence of operational change. It helps establish whether the business is adding locations, distribution capacity or other infrastructure that could increase financial coordination requirements.
+
+**The intended output is not a list of companies with finance vacancies or expansion announcements. It is a pipeline of ICP-qualified accounts with verified events, relevant buying roles and a defensible commercial angle.**
+
+---
+
+## 3. ICP Filter
+
+### 3.1 Approved ICP scope
+
+The workflow must cover all seven segments defined in `icp.md`, retaining their original priority classifications.
+
+| Priority | Segment | Segment code | ICP qualification basis |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| PRIMARY | FMCG Distributors and Wholesalers | `FMCG_DISTRIBUTOR` | Multiple depots or significant warehousing, plus trade receivables or credit-based sales |
+| PRIMARY | Petrol Station Networks | `PETROL_STATION_NETWORK` | Owns or manages at least 3 stations and performs daily cash/POS reconciliation |
```

```diff
 The workflow evaluates the two signals independently.
 
-| Detected evidence                                   | Decision                          | Treatment                                                                                      |

-| Verified finance recruitment only                   | `FINANCE_ONLY`                    | Preferred-signal account; route using the finance-recruitment angle                            |
-| Verified expansion only                             | `EXPANSION_ONLY`                  | Supporting-signal account; route using the segment-specific expansion angle                    |
-| Both signals independently verified                 | `BOTH`                            | Higher research priority; investigate whether finance responsibilities relate to the expansion |
-| Neither selected signal detected                    | `NONE`                            | Retain as ICP-qualified; do not present as signal-qualified                                    |
-| Signal detected but not attributable to the account | `RESEARCH_REQUIRED`               | Do not route as verified                                                                       |
-| Signal detected but event cannot be verified        | `RESEARCH_REQUIRED` or `REJECTED` | Resolve the evidence gap or reject the event                                                   |
-| Signal detected at an ICP-disqualified account      | No qualified routing              | Preserve research evidence if useful, but exclude from qualified pipeline                      |
+| Detected evidence | Decision | Treatment |
+|---|---|---|

+| Verified expansion only | `EXPANSION_ONLY` | Supporting-signal account; route using the segment-specific expansion angle |
+| Both signals independently verified | `BOTH` | Higher research priority; investigate whether finance responsibilities relate to the expansion |
+| Neither selected signal detected | `NONE` | Retain as ICP-qualified; do not present as signal-qualified |
```

@@ -2,214 +2,449 @@
 
 ## 1. System Objective
 
-Design a GTM system that identifies, researches, qualifies and prioritises Nigerian businesses with potential banking, payment and financial operations needs.
-
-**Initial target segments:**
-
-- Retail supermarket chains (`RETAIL_SUPERMARKET`)
-- FMCG distributors (`FMCG_DISTRIBUTOR`)
-
-The initial implementation is restricted to these two segments. Fuel and service stations, general retail and service SMEs, and other unrelated business categories are outside the approved workflow scope.
-
-Initial commercial prioritisation focuses on retail chains and FMCG distributors, subject to evidence of ICP fit and operational relevance.
-
-The system uses publicly available company information and observable buying signals to produce evidence-backed prospecting recommendations.
-
-ICP definitions, customer pain points, buying signals and qualification thresholds are treated as research hypotheses until validated. Moniepoint product capabilities must be verified against authoritative sources.
+Design and implement an evidence-led GTM system that discovers, qualifies, researches and prioritises Nigerian businesses within Moniepoint's approved Ideal Customer Profile (ICP).
+
+The system identifies qualified businesses, detects relevant commercial events, researches buying roles and produces evidence-backed account records with defensible commercial angles for human review.
+
+### Approved ICP scope
+
+The system covers all seven segments defined in `icp.md`, retaining their approved priority classifications.
+
+| Priority | Segment | Segment code |
+|---|---|---|
+| PRIMARY | FMCG Distributors and Wholesalers | `FMCG_DISTRIBUTOR` |
+| PRIMARY | Petrol Station Networks | `PETROL_STATION_NETWORK` |
+| PRIMARY | Retail Chains (Supermarkets) | `RETAIL_SUPERMARKET` |
+| SECONDARY | Quick-Service Restaurant Groups | `QSR_GROUP` |
+| SECONDARY | Pharmacy Chains | `PHARMACY_CHAIN` |
+| EMERGING | Logistics and Haulage | `LOGISTICS_HAULAGE` |
+| EMERGING | Specialized Healthcare and Diagnostic Networks | `HEALTHCARE_DIAGNOSTICS` |
+
+Segment priority determines campaign and research priority. It does not establish ICP qualification.
+
+The initial campaign priority remains the three PRIMARY segments. SECONDARY and EMERGING segments remain within the approved research universe but must retain their classifications in the system. They must not be silently promoted into the primary campaign cohort.
+
+### Selected buying signals
+
+The system operates around two selected signals:
+
+- **Preferred signal:** `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT`
+- **Supporting signal:** `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION`
+
+Finance recruitment is the principal detection and routing signal. Expansion provides supporting evidence that can strengthen prioritisation or independently support a segment-specific commercial angle.
+
+Neither signal can override ICP qualification.
+
+The system uses publicly available company information and observable business events. Material account and signal decisions must retain their supporting evidence and research provenance.
+
+ICP definitions, signal interpretations and commercial value propositions must follow their respective authority documents. Product capabilities and eligibility must not be assumed from ICP fit or signal detection.
+
+---
 
 ## 2. End-to-End Architecture
 
-The system consists of ten operational stages:
-
-The system consists of ten operational stages:
-
-1. Account discovery
-2. Business identity verification and deduplication
-3. Mandatory ICP qualification
-4. Company-level enrichment and evidence validation
-5. Independent finance recruitment and multi-branch expansion research
-6. Signal outcome assessment and account routing
-7. Buyer identification and persona classification
-8. Clay-based prospect preparation and contact enrichment
-9. Human review and approval
-10. CRM preparation and feedback
+The architecture consists of ten operational stages.
+
+1. Account discovery.
+2. Business identity verification and deduplication.
+3. Mandatory ICP qualification.
+4. Company enrichment and ICP evidence validation.
+5. Preferred finance recruitment detection and verification.
+6. Supporting expansion detection and verification.
+7. Signal assessment, account prioritisation and commercial routing.
+8. Account and buying-role enrichment, including Clay preparation.
+9. Human review and outreach approval.
+10. CRM preparation and feedback.
+
+The architecture follows the workflow sequence:
+
+`ACCOUNT DISCOVERY → ICP QUALIFICATION → FINANCE RECRUITMENT DETECTION → FINANCE RECRUITMENT VERIFICATION → EXPANSION DETECTION → EXPANSION VERIFICATION → SIGNAL SCORING → ACCOUNT AND PERSONA ENRICHMENT → COMMERCIAL ROUTING → HUMAN REVIEW → OUTREACH APPROVAL`

-The two signal searches are independent. Both must reach terminal outcomes before final signal routing. An unresolved search must not be treated as NOT_DETECTED.
+**Architecture rules:**
+
+- ICP qualification precedes signal detection.
+- `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT` is the preferred signal and is researched first.
+- `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION` is researched after the preferred signal and provides supporting evidence.
+- Each signal has its own detection outcome, verification outcome and confidence assessment.
+- An unresolved signal search must never be treated as `NOT_DETECTED`.
+- An account with no verified selected signal can remain ICP-qualified for future monitoring.
+- Only ICP-qualified accounts enter qualified signal prioritisation.
+- Commercial routing must use the verified segment and actual signal event.
+- Human review and outreach approval remain separate controls.
+
+---
 
 ## 3. Core System Components
 
-| Component              | Responsibility                                                                                               |
-| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
-| Context OS             | Maintains company context, ICP definitions, buying signals and operating instructions.                       |
-| Account discovery      | Identifies businesses within the two approved target segments.                                               |
-| Company enrichment     | Establishes identity, Nigerian operations, physical operating model and other mandatory ICP evidence.        |
-| Signal detection       | Independently investigates finance recruitment and multi-branch expansion after mandatory ICP qualification. |
-| ICP qualification      | Evaluates the six mandatory conditions using PASS, FAIL or UNKNOWN.                                          |
-| Account prioritisation | Applies deterministic routing using both signal outcomes, evidence quality and commercial relevance.         |
-| Buyer identification   | Identifies relevant decision-makers and influencers after account qualification and routing.                 |
-| Clay                   | Supports account enrichment, signal research, contact preparation and workflow execution.                    |
-| Human review           | Validates evidence, qualification, routing and contact readiness before approval.                            |
-| Feedback               | Improves future research and qualification criteria.                                                         |
+| Component | Responsibility |
+|---|---|
+| Context OS | Maintains approved ICP definitions, signal specifications, persona guidance and system instructions. |
+| Account discovery | Identifies potential businesses across the seven approved ICP segments. |
+| Identity verification | Establishes company identity, Nigerian operating presence and relevant operating entity. |
+| ICP qualification | Evaluates general qualification requirements, segment-specific mandatory conditions and anti-ICP exclusions. |
+| Company enrichment | Captures operating model, locations and evidence needed to support ICP decisions. |
+| Preferred signal detection | Searches for relevant finance, treasury, financial control, accounting and finance operations vacancies. |
+| Supporting signal detection | Searches for segment-specific expansion events and operational developments. |
+| Signal verification | Establishes event attribution, source credibility, event status and relevance. |
+| Account prioritisation | Applies the approved hierarchy using ICP priority and independently verified signal outcomes. |
+| Account and persona enrichment | Identifies relevant decision-makers, influencers and functional buying roles. |
+| Commercial routing | Assigns the correct segment-specific angle based on verified events. |
+| Clay | Supports account enrichment, linked signal evidence, contact preparation and workflow execution. |
+| Human review | Checks qualification, evidence, attribution, routing and contact readiness. |
+| Outreach approval | Controls whether an approved account and contact can proceed to outreach. |
+| CRM preparation | Produces approved records for subsequent CRM processing. |
+| Feedback | Captures research outcomes and operational findings to improve future execution. |
+
+---
 
 ## 4. Qualification and Prioritisation Logic
 
-The system distinguishes between two independent assessments:
-
-**ICP fit:** How closely does the business match the target customer profile?
-
-**Buying intent:** What observable evidence suggests a relevant business need or reason to engage?
-
-**ICP fit:** Whether the business satisfies the six mandatory qualification conditions defined in `workflow.md`.
-
-Each condition receives one of three outcomes:
-
-- PASS
-- FAIL
-- UNKNOWN
-
-Overall qualification:
-
-- `QUALIFIED`: All six conditions PASS.
-- `DISQUALIFIED`: At least one condition definitively FAILS.
-- `FURTHER_RESEARCH`: No condition FAILS, but at least one remains UNKNOWN.
-
-Missing evidence must not be treated as a negative finding.
-
-Buying signals are restricted to two approved categories:
-
-- `FINANCE_RECRUITMENT`: Relevant finance, treasury, accounting or financial operations recruitment.
-- `MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION`: Verified or attributable expansion of physical retail or distribution operations.
-
-Warehouse, logistics and operations recruitment may be retained as supplementary research observations, but must not operate as an independent buying signal or influence current routing.
-
-### Preliminary qualification outcomes
-
-| ICP outcome        | Interpretation                                                    |
-| ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
-| `QUALIFIED`        | All six mandatory ICP conditions have passed.                     |
-| `DISQUALIFIED`     | At least one mandatory ICP condition has definitively failed.     |
-| `FURTHER_RESEARCH` | No definitive failure, but one or more conditions remain unknown. |
-
-### Fit Tier and Signal Routing
-
-Fit tier is assessed separately from mandatory ICP qualification:
-
-- `EXCELLENT_FIT`
-- `REASONABLE_FIT`
-- `UNASSESSED`
-
-Five or more physical locations is a prioritisation heuristic, not a mandatory qualification threshold. A qualifying single-location supermarket may still be included. FMCG distributors do not need retail outlets if their distribution operations are independently verified.
-
-Signal outcomes are assessed independently:
-
-- `DETECTED`
-- `NOT_DETECTED`
-- `UNVERIFIED`
-- `HISTORICAL`
... diff truncated ...

```diff
 # Moniepoint GTM Systems OS
 
-An evidence-led GTM Engineering project exploring how business intelligence, ICP research, buying signals and structured workflows can support customer acquisition for Moniepoint Business Banking and Payments in Nigeria.
+An evidence-led GTM Engineering project exploring how business intelligence, Ideal Customer Profile (ICP) research, buying signals, company enrichment and structured workflows can support customer acquisition for Moniepoint Business Banking and Payments in Nigeria.
 
 **Market:** Nigeria  
-**Focus:** Retail chains, FMCG distributors and multi-location businesses  
-**Status:** Research automation and systems design foundation
+**Product Focus:** Business banking, merchant payments, collections and settlements  
+**Primary Campaign Cohort:** FMCG distributors, petrol station networks and retail chains/supermarkets  
+**Project Status:** Research automation, ICP and signal intelligence completed; integrated GTM system under development
 
 ## Project Overview
 
-The objective is to develop a structured system for identifying, researching, qualifying and prioritising businesses with potential banking and payment needs.
-
-The project connects:
-
-- Ideal Customer Profile (ICP) research
-- Buying-signal identification
-- Company enrichment and account qualification
-- Account prioritisation
-- Decision-maker research
+The objective is to design and progressively implement an integrated GTM intelligence and execution system for identifying, researching, qualifying and prioritising businesses with potential banking and payment needs.
+
+The system connects market research, account intelligence, observable business developments, company and contact enrichment, qualification workflows and CRM automation to produce evidence-backed prospect recommendations and structured sales preparation.
+
+The project is organised around:
+
+- Market and ICP research
+- Segment-specific business qualification
+- Buying-signal identification and verification
+- Company and source enrichment
+- Account prioritisation and routing
+- Decision-maker and buying-committee research
+- CRM integration and lifecycle management
+- Workflow automation
 - Prospect preparation and human review
 
+The architecture separates ICP qualification from buying-signal detection. A business must first meet the applicable ICP requirements before its buying signals influence account prioritisation.
+
+## ICP and Campaign Scope
+
+The market research process investigates a broad range of Nigerian business sectors to identify commercially relevant opportunities for Moniepoint.
+
+The approved ICP defines seven segments across three priority classifications.
+
+| Priority | Approved Segments |
+| --- | --- |
+| Primary | FMCG distributors and wholesalers |
+| Primary | Petrol station networks |
+| Primary | Retail chains and supermarkets |
+| Secondary | Quick-service restaurant groups |
+| Secondary | Pharmacy chains |
+| Emerging | Logistics and haulage businesses |
+| Emerging | Specialised healthcare and diagnostic networks |
+
+The primary campaign cohort consists of the three primary segments. Secondary and emerging segments remain within the approved research universe but are not automatically included in the primary campaign.
+
+The project distinguishes between:
+
+- **ICP fit:** Whether a business meets the applicable customer qualification criteria.
+- **Buying signals:** Observable developments that may indicate a relevant commercial opportunity.
+- **Prioritisation:** How qualified accounts are ranked using their evidence, signals and commercial relevance.
+- **Contact readiness:** Whether sufficient company and decision-maker information exists to prepare a prospect for human review.
+
+A missing public buying signal does not automatically disqualify an otherwise qualified business.
+
+## Buying-Signal Framework
+
+The preferred buying signal is **FINANCE_RECRUITMENT**, with **MULTI_BRANCH_EXPANSION** as a supporting signal.
+
+The workflow investigates finance and treasury recruitment as a potential indicator of changing financial-management requirements, organisational complexity or investment in financial controls.
+
+Expansion is treated as supporting context. New branches, stations, depots and outlets may increase the relevance of a finance recruitment signal, but expansion alone does not establish purchasing intent.
+
+Signal detection, evidence verification, confidence assessment and account routing are treated as separate steps.
+
+## Planned System Capabilities
+
+The intended system extends beyond research automation into an integrated GTM intelligence and execution workflow.
+
+The complete system is designed to connect:
+
+1. **Market and ICP Intelligence:** Identify commercially relevant business segments and define evidence-backed account qualification criteria.
+2. **Buying-Signal Intelligence:** Detect and assess observable business developments, including finance recruitment and multi-branch expansion.
+3. **Company Enrichment:** Build structured company records using public business information and enrichment providers.
+4. **Account Qualification:** Evaluate businesses against the approved ICP, applicable segment requirements and exclusion criteria.
+5. **Signal Assessment and Prioritisation:** Associate verified buying signals with qualified accounts and determine their relative commercial priority.
+6. **Audience Segmentation and Routing:** Classify accounts and route them through workflows according to ICP fit, signal evidence and qualification status.
+7. **Decision-Maker Enrichment:** Identify relevant economic buyers, finance stakeholders and other members of the buying committee.
+8. **CRM Integration:** Transfer approved account and contact records into HubSpot and organise them within a structured sales pipeline.
+9. **Workflow Automation:** Connect Clay, HubSpot and n8n to support enrichment, qualification, record updates and workflow execution.
+10. **Sales Preparation and Human Review:** Produce structured prospect intelligence and support controlled progression towards sales engagement.
+
+These capabilities describe the intended end-to-end system. Their implementation is tracked separately below.
+
 ## Repository Structure

-|---|---|
+| --- | --- |
 | `CLAUDE.md` | Project context and operating instructions |
-| `icp/` | ICP and buying-signal research |
-| `research/` | Structured research sources and provenance |
-| `tools/` | Python and Gemini research automation |
-| `architecture.md` | Proposed GTM systems architecture |
-| `workflow.md` | Account qualification workflow |
-| `.claude/skills/` | Project-specific ICP evaluation skill |
```
